# Scout build tasks

Build a proof of concept that collects BBNJ events, announcements, and treaty-status changes; stores source-backed records; and makes them available through Slack and a filterable web list. Target recurring service fees: **$0/month**.

**Controlling pre-launch data policy — 2026-10-04:** the user says all Scout content is disposable testing content until launch. Records, captures, assessments and collection-history/report bodies may be cleared/recollected when needed for the rebuild, without another confirmation. This supersedes older preservation/no-reset requirements, including SCOUT-17's original wording. Dated historical proofs remain history, not current acceptance constraints; do not build old-content migration, compatibility or reconstructed reports. Preserve tracker/source configuration, Auth/access, credentials, service setup, unrelated shared-project data and accounting required to enforce current model limits. Never reset quota usage to gain allowance. Correctness and inspectability within fresh runs remain required. See SCOUT-17 for the reset boundary/timing; this policy update itself performs no wipe.

## Sources and scope

- [BBNJ Scout Letter](https://docs.google.com/document/d/13HIEN45IEKQN-i29pQuoRD-eeFOl-t70a1_2TBpV7zI/edit): product brief, Features, and Services.
- [BBNJ Scout Trackers](https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1VtpaC83ybg7zet7VvNXmvctN9s5WJK_NTlJ9wx5NIbM/edit): source inventory, collection methods, and cadences; 22 is the last imported app inventory, not a claim about the sheet's current row count. Read the live sheet before any authorized import.
- This file is the authoritative implementation checklist. The letter remains the product brief. Resolve material conflicts with the user before implementing the affected behavior.
- Confirmed POC scope: events, announcements, treaty-status changes, daily Slack findings, a weekly Slack digest, Slack questions, a searchable Records table/card view, a tracker table with derived collection health, and tracker management.
- Current deployed implementation: descriptions capped at 280 characters, UTC display/query/digest dates, and four tables/46 columns through migration 20261001190000: trackers, collection runs, records and evidence. Evidence links captures directly to records; source history and a communication timestamp replace the record-edit/delivery logs. Full/date-only/treaty evidence stays in captures. Backend-controlled Auth metadata grants admin access only to an eligible Workspace identity. SCOUT-15 core, minimal communication state and four additional collectors are deployed and reviewed; source coverage remains partial.
- Calendar generation/actions and native calendar import are out of scope by explicit user choice, not pending verification. Outbound email digests and a separate calendar view remain deferred.
- [Email subscriptions planning tab](https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1VtpaC83ybg7zet7VvNXmvctN9s5WJK_NTlJ9wx5NIbM/edit?gid=20261001#gid=20261001): four starter subscriptions were Planned with mailbox blank at the approved checkpoint. This is inbound-source planning only; subscription signup, mailbox ingestion and outbound email delivery are not implemented or authorized by this reconciliation. Further sheet edits do not change the last imported count of 22.
- Deferred: embeddings and semantic search. Use structured queries for the POC.

## Working rules

- Implement only when the user requests implementation. Creating this checklist does not start the build.
- The user authorized milestone 1 (SCOUT-01–06) with manager chat `01a0f59f-1e17-7f70-beb9-6dc786ecd7cb` assigning/reviewing one worker. The manager heartbeat is paused. Later user-directed chats have their own explicit scopes; this reconciliation neither restarts them nor expands milestone authorization. Stop for review after an assignment; SCOUT-07+ remain unstarted unless separately authorized. Coordinate writers in this shared checkout.
- **2026-10-02 authorization:** the user requested the Enabled definition/data change and a stepped plan for every tracker, handed to the existing manager for implementation. SCOUT-16 below authorizes that collector sequence, its tests, bounded live proof, scoped deployment and evidence-based promotion. It supersedes older stop-after-expansion limits for this work. The manager may assign/review the existing worker in this same checkout. Scheduling, heartbeat restart, Slack sends, mailbox signup/ingestion, paid services and new tracker imports remain separate decisions.
- After two unsuccessful approaches or roughly 20 minutes without new evidence, reassess the specific failure and move to independent work; do not automatically hand off or repeat an unchanged failure. Check prior chats/documentation and try materially different supported paths. Respect actual authorization denials and sandbox boundaries. Request user action only for an essential missing capability/decision after authorized alternatives and useful independent work are exhausted. Keep unverified criteria open. Keep the collector sequence; add no upfront feasibility or backup work.
- Record material changes and deviations under the affected task: date, change, reason, verification/gap, and deferred work's revisit condition. Manager-approved independent work may proceed before a dependency is fully complete; dependent deployment/integration, source evidence, correctness, and the $0 constraint remain required.
- Use statuses `todo`, `in-progress`, `blocked`, and `done`. Keep task IDs stable. Start with the first task whose dependencies are complete.
- Keep changes minimal, follow existing patterns, and avoid speculative helpers, scripts, abstractions, or cleanup.
- Work in the supplied local checkout. Do not clone, create a worktree, or change checkouts unless explicitly requested.
- Before marking a task done, check every acceptance criterion, record verification and material limitations, and create one focused commit for the implemented task unless instructed otherwise.
- Record blockers and the specific missing input. Continue independent authorized work where possible. Do not treat unsupported coverage as successful collection.
- Use small recorded source examples for repeatable checks of parsing, deduplication, dates, and failures; use a bounded live check to confirm each integration. Do not add tests that merely mirror implementation.

## Shared constraints

- Use recurring free tiers. Confirm current account eligibility and limits during setup; quoted allowances below are planning assumptions from the Services tab.
- Keep paid upgrades and automatic top-ups off. Apply model and search budgets from their first use, including retries. Stop at the configured limit and record quota exhaustion in collection runs; derive tracker status from those runs.
- Preserve collected records when a source fails or a quota is exhausted. Distinguish successful collection with no changes from failed or partial collection.
- Use source links and collection timestamps for every record. Keep missing dates, time zones, or other unsupported facts unknown. Model output is not evidence.
- Extract and summarize only new or changed content; reuse stored results. Send only public source material and the specific Scout question to the proposed free model service.
- Keep collection and notification schedules independent. Trackers retain one cadence interval: daily Bluesky feeds, mostly weekly websites, weekly discovery. Meeting-period notes were intentionally removed and are not operative schedule configuration. Notification time, weekday and time zone still need a decision; UTC event display does not decide them.
- Import the sheet once to seed trackers. The app owns subsequent edits; do not introduce continuing sheet synchronization.
- **Enabled = proven collector:** Yes requires a working, tested collector with actual received/persisted source data for the exact configuration. No means unconfigured, unimplemented, unproven or invalidated proof. It is not a pause flag, schedule or complete-coverage claim. New trackers default to No. Use SCOUT-16's validation/promotion gate; historical references to Enabled as a pause switch are superseded.
- Keep credentials server-side. Latest accepted access direction (2026-10-01): retain Firebase Hosting Spark with a public static login shell/assets and private Scout data/actions. Any Google-verified `oceanagentics.com` Workspace user receives reader access on first sign-in, without individual invitations/passwords or manually provisioned memberships. Verify Google's signed hosted-domain assertion on a trusted server path; a client `hd` hint, email suffix or editable metadata is insufficient. Supabase independently enforces data/API access. Admin elevation and backend collector access remain denied by default. This supersedes the earlier whole-frontend IAM gateway hold. No Cloudflare, IAP, Cloud Run, hosting migration or billing upgrade.
- Store only supported event instants; render and query them in UTC. Preserve date-only/unknown precision in source captures rather than inventing a time or restoring removed record columns. Keep source publication, capture, last-seen and record-version times distinct.

## Milestones

1. **First useful flow — SCOUT-01 through SCOUT-06:** one source produces stored records, a usable private web list with source links, and integrated manual Slack delivery.
2. **Scheduled coverage — SCOUT-07 through SCOUT-11:** due trackers run automatically, failures are visible, and official sources and discovery expand coverage.
3. **Complete POC — SCOUT-12 through SCOUT-14:** Slack questions, tracker management, and verified end-to-end behavior.

## SCOUT-02 — Create the app and database foundation

Status: in-progress
Depends on: SCOUT-01

Outcome: A minimal app runs locally and on Firebase Hosting, with Supabase project `chm` and versioned migrations for its `scout` schema.

Acceptance criteria:

- [x] Select a minimal stack with a static frontend deployable to Firebase Hosting on Spark and a Supabase backend; record local, migration, and deployment commands. Reuse the Ryu hosting approach for a separate Scout site without changing Ryu's deployment. Keep workers and schedules on Supabase; Firebase Cloud Functions/Blaze and a Sheets/Apps Script backend redesign are outside this POC plan.
- [x] A deployed app can read a harmless sample record from the database. Verify local startup and the hosted URL.
- [x] Create the `scout` schema in `chm`'s default `postgres` database. Put Scout's trackers, collection history, events, announcements, treaty-status changes, source references, and notification delivery state there (for example, `scout.events`). Use the smallest schema that supports these responsibilities.
- [x] Configure clients and migrations to target `scout` explicitly. Add `scout` to the Data API's exposed schemas while preserving existing services; grant only required schema/table/function privileges and enable RLS with policies in the same migrations. Do not grant automatic access to all future tables.
- [x] Implement the approved lean record locally: title/type/organization, description ≤280 characters, event links/location/status, supported start/end instants and record version. Keep full text, publication/date-only/treaty facts in source captures; do not restore duplicate record columns.
- [x] Keep source publication, capture, last-seen and record-version times distinct. Link captures directly to records, copying shared captures per record. Preserve source and delivery history; omit the separate record-edit log by user direction.
- [x] Enforce Scout-specific access within the project's shared Auth setup. Verify unauthorized writes and access to private records across service boundaries fail; authentication in another service does not confer Scout administrator access. Keep project-wide service-role credentials backend-only and absent from browser assets and logs.
- [x] Google-verified `oceanagentics.com` Workspace users obtain hosted reader access automatically. Deny Gmail, other/no domains, unverified identities, forged metadata/tokens, anonymous requests and unauthorized writes. Preserve shared Auth/provider/redirect settings and default-denied administrator/collector access; remove rendered data on sign-out. Distinguish synthetic policy tests from real Google login.
- [x] Review existing migration history before applying Scout migrations. Verify they succeed in an empty development database and preserve other services' schema objects, data, and permissions in a shared development database. Never reset the shared remote project as a setup step.
- [x] Deploy the four cleanup migrations (`20261001150000` through `20261001180000`) with the matching collector/frontend. Verify five tables, preserved captures/deliveries, `tracker_status`/`source_details`, unchanged other-service controls and SQL access under the existing verified Workspace identity. Signed-in browser verification remains in SCOUT-05. Unmatched/conflicting source evidence must stop migration for reconciliation.
- [x] Verify the deployed replacement of memberships with backend-controlled `raw_app_meta_data.scout.admin`; user-editable metadata cannot grant admin, and verified Workspace identity remains required. Preserve existing explicit admins without provisioning a new one.

Current status: the four-table schema is deployed; all eleven migration bodies match local SQL. The separately authorized SCOUT-15 reset and fresh records are documented below. The existing Workspace identity has reader access, zero tracker writes and no record-write/raw-evidence privileges. No admin was provisioned and other-service controls are unchanged.

Historical verification and deviations (2026-10-01):

- **Implemented:** Vite + vanilla JavaScript with the Supabase client explicitly targeting `scout`; in-memory sessions, no signup or automatic membership, and a closed setup state without public configuration. Added minimal ignores, public-only environment example, scoped Firebase target, and development/migration/deployment instructions in README. Secrets stay out of source and build assets. One typed `records` table covers the three record kinds to keep the schema small; six tables include memberships, trackers, collection runs, records, sources, and deliveries. No tracker import or collector work started.
- **Authorization:** The migration enables RLS and revokes/regrants only its fixed table set in the same transaction. Anonymous access is denied; authenticated users need explicit Scout membership. Readers can read evidence; admins can edit tracker configuration but cannot forge collection-success fields, grant membership, or write evidence/delivery state. Backend service-role access remains project-wide and privileged. The migration rejects a pre-existing `scout` schema and unsafe global client table defaults rather than changing another service's privileges.
- **Local checks:** `npm test` passes three PostgreSQL suites: empty database, shared-service fixture, and unsafe-global-default rejection. Coverage includes a harmless sample, multiple sources, null/invalid dates, unauthorized reads/writes, admin limitations, backend writes, private future tables, and unchanged other-service data/ownership/grants/policies. `npm run build` passes both without configuration and with a local fixture public key. Browser checks verify local startup, the closed setup state, and configured sign-in failure without granting access. No hosted Auth/PostgREST success is claimed.
- **Local test deviation:** Native PostgreSQL 16 initialization failed twice (including approved elevated execution) at shared-memory creation; Docker is not running. Use the documented PGlite in-memory PostgreSQL runtime as a dev-only dependency for repeatable SQL/RLS tests, with minimal `auth.users`/`auth.uid()` fixtures. This verifies database behavior, not the full Supabase platform. Revisit hosted integration after remote authentication/state checks.
- **Hosting complete:** Created separate site `bbnj-scout` under Spark project `ryustatic`, mapped target `scout`, and deployed [the static shell](https://bbnj-scout.web.app) through the documented Hosting REST API using the existing saved Firebase login refreshed in memory. Release `sites/bbnj-scout/releases/1790829822838000`, version `b04336502bfe48fb`; three compressed files total 1,984 bytes. Predeploy checks confirmed billing disabled and latest sampled usage of 11,045,338 stored bytes / 55,432,079 monthly transfer bytes. Hosted HTML/CSS/JS return HTTP 200, exactly match `dist`, and carry the configured CSP/security headers. Browser rendering verified. Ryu release `sites/ryustatic/releases/1790266049490000` is unchanged.
- **Supabase evidence:** The previously successful functions check remains valid historical evidence. The linked-query CLI currently fails an IPv6 connection preflight even after its supported relink reports success. Official CLI source confirms that this preflight runs before its Management API query. Source review also established the documented native credential service/account names; native retrieval did not yield a token. The manager subsequently verified that bounded functions reads now return `Access token not provided` both normally and with approved elevated execution. No more interactive credential prompts or Keychain permission changes will be attempted while the user is away.
- **Exact unattended prerequisite:** Make an existing authorized Supabase token available noninteractively through the documented CLI credential store or `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN`, explicitly loaded into the CLI/API process through an approved secret loader. An entry in `.env.local` alone is insufficient. Do not broaden token permissions or expose its value. First verify a bounded project read without stdin/prompts, then inspect the actual database/migration history and shared plan/capacity. If the IPv6 preflight persists, use the documented Management API with that same authorized access. No remote SQL, migration, Data API change, membership assignment, or public-key retrieval was completed.
- **Remaining boundary:** SCOUT-02 is partial, not done. The local migration and policy tests are ready; remote schema creation, preserving/appending Data API exposure, remote permission checks, and the hosted sample read remain unchecked. The deployed shell makes no database requests until configured. Reader/admin identities remain denied by default pending an explicit decision. Revisit these checks when unattended authentication is available; SCOUT-03 still waits for manager review. Previously approved Tavily/scheduling deferrals remain unchanged.

Historical implementation and verification (2026-10-01):

- **Manual local path:** Added `npm run notify` for an explicit historical baseline, preparing at most five findings/material changes, one historical verification example, an explicit-window digest, payload review, individual sends and inspected receipts. It uses the existing persistent local PostgreSQL database and collector lock; collection and delivery state stay separate. A versioned migration adds exact payload/record snapshots, attempt times and receipt evidence to backend-only deliveries without changing browser grants. No new package, remote database write, collector run, model/Tavily call or scheduler was needed.
- **Source and date behavior:** Reused the record/date module. Messages distinguish event, publication, collection and update dates, show source links, and include before/after material changes. Digests omit cancelled/past events, preserve source-local window boundaries and show unknown dates separately, with record/coverage counts. At most five dated and three unknown entries appear; all qualifying version evidence is retained. The same inclusive window returns the same payload. Prepared records that change or cross event timing states fail before send. Source prose uses plain-text blocks and links disable mention parsing/unfurling.
- **Durable outcomes:** Persist exact payloads and version evidence before network I/O. A unique version/window key suppresses completed repeats. Save `sending` before each attempt; timeout, network failure, 5xx, unexpected response and interrupted sends become `unknown`, requiring inspection. Only HTTP 429 retries once, respecting a valid `Retry-After` of at most 30 seconds; every delivery is capped at two attempts. Other 4xx responses fail without reposting. Requests have a 12-second deadline and 4 KiB response limit. HTTP 200 `ok` means accepted; a separate receipt records the inspected Slack timestamp and evidence note. Unknown delivery for a record blocks later notifications until reconciled.
- **Historical baseline:** Created baseline `1cf18543-e3f1-455c-a3c2-cdc07b0e93cc` covering all 12 existing records before notification. Repeating it returns that baseline; preparing ordinary findings returns zero. Tests demonstrate that later new records and material changes remain eligible, while collection-only provenance refreshes do not notify.

The transport rejects other bindings. After all 20 tests passed and stored payloads were reviewed, sent exactly one labelled historical HSA finding, delivery `3d0e7455-4bf0-4ceb-a4a5-5cf3e318c2f7`, [Slack message `1790834126.338919`](https://oceanagentics.slack.com/archives/C0C5PE21KN1/p1790834126338919), and one labelled October 1–7 digest, delivery `7dea4bc5-05ba-4196-8e0d-b18ef673da3d`, [Slack message `1790834129.395769`](https://oceanagentics.slack.com/archives/C0C5PE21KN1/p1790834129395769). Both were accepted on attempt 1. Repeating both send commands kept attempts at 1 and produced no duplicate. Slack readback confirms both UUIDs, installation, message content/date labels and source links; timestamps and inspection notes are persisted. The digest honestly has zero upcoming and zero unknown-date events, covering one successfully collected tracker out of 22. No synthetic event facts were posted.
- **Verification scope:** All 20 tests pass, including baseline/new/change eligibility, source-safe formatting, local date boundaries, past/cancelled/ongoing/unknown digest selection, empty results, frozen/stale payload behavior, backend-only delivery access, a 429→success retry with identical payloads, exhausted/permanent rejection, network/5xx/unexpected-response uncertainty, interrupted sends and explicit receipt reconciliation. This tests local PostgreSQL plus the two real Slack receipts; hosted execution remains unverified.

Existing Slack installation scopes have not been independently audited; no new scopes were requested and only the existing webhook sends. Visual browser inspection was rejected by automatic approval review because it could not establish permission to view the private channel. The manager explicitly approved deferring this optional check; no retry or alternate route was attempted. Connector readback verifies receipt and structured content, not visual layout. The earlier optional browser-permission question is not a prerequisite for this delivery. Keep the combined rendering/baseline criterion open until permitted browser or user inspection. Supabase actual Free-plan/paid-add-on verification, member identities, remote integration and the approved native calendar-save check remain deferred. Historical focused correction after manager review (2026-10-01):

- **Problem and change:** The manager reproduced an empty prepared digest becoming stale after a new event was collected: sending correctly failed before I/O, but the fixed window key kept returning that failed payload. Added explicit `npm run notify -- reprepare STALE_DELIVERY_ID`, limited to that exact stale failure with zero attempts and no send/receipt timestamps. It creates a new pending payload for the same window or current record, with a `replaces` link and a unique replacement key. The original row, key, payload, snapshot and failure evidence remain unchanged. Existing preparation requests follow the replacement chain, and record-version suppression includes replacement snapshots. No new migration, package, automatic retry or force reset was added.
- **Review and deduplication:** Recovery only prepares; the operator must review the returned new ID before a separate send. Repeating recovery of an old ID returns the latest existing replacement. A replacement that itself expires requires an explicit request using that newer stale ID. Pending, baseline, sending, unknown, sent and attempted failures cannot be replaced. Another delivery for the current record version or an uncertain record outcome must be reviewed first. Attempted 4xx failures retain the existing manual-review limitation.
- **Verification:** All eight notifier suites pass using injected transports and in-memory databases with the actual migrations. Three new regressions cover the newly populated same-window digest, timing-only findings across Upcoming → In progress → Past and later material corrections, immutable original evidence, replacement chains, repeated completion producing exactly one send, and refusal to replace protected/attempted states. No further live Slack messages or remote database operations were performed. The CLI syntax check and staged whitespace/credential checks pass.
- **Remaining boundary:** Manager review of the original live receipt/content, 12-record baseline, repeat-send results and 20-test run is recorded. Slack visual layout and native calendar import remain approved deferrals; hosted calendar links, remote Supabase plan/member prerequisites and hosted integration remain unfinished. ## SCOUT-09 — Collect authoritative treaty-status changes

Status: done (bounded implementation accepted and exact tracker promoted after manager review)
Depends on: SCOUT-03, SCOUT-04, SCOUT-15, SCOUT-16.01; SCOUT-07 for scheduling only.

Outcome: Changes in the UN Treaty Collection become source-backed country/action/date records.

Acceptance criteria:

- [x] Collect the official participant record identified in the tracker inventory and preserve the four currently reported action types. Unknown future actions/suffixes fail visibly rather than being guessed.
- [x] Establish an initial baseline without announcing all historical entries as new. Later verified additions or corrections produce appropriate changes.
- [x] Preserve country, action and date at their original precision in official source captures and the concise record representation; keep capture/publication times distinct. Query those facts in evidence JSON without restoring country/action/date record columns. Treat NGO/social reports as leads until verified officially.
- [x] A failed or incomplete fetch cannot imply a reversal or deletion of a country's status.
- [x] Verify initial baseline, a new action, a correction, repeated collection, and incomplete source protection.

Implementation/proof checkpoint: **2026-10-02 local / 2026-10-03 UTC**. Existing seed 0 tracker **`fa465a3b-c9d3-4810-9efb-cd6ab9dcc49c`**, collector **`web`**, URL **`https://treaties.un.org/Pages/ViewDetails.aspx?src=TREATY&mtdsg_no=XXI-10&chapter=21&clang=_en`**, cadence **7 days**, was **Enabled No throughout proof**. No configuration field changed during validation; the later reviewed promotion is recorded below. The existing private collector now recognizes this exact configuration; normal disabled runs still reject it, while `validate=true` performs the bounded proof. No new table, record columns, scraper framework, secret or frontend deployment was added.

Source comparison: direct bounded HTTP captures of the configured HTML (**314,063 bytes**) and its observed XML publication (**78,477 bytes**) both report **102 parties / 145 signatories**. All **149** country/signature/action cells match between these fresh captures, including **Mozambique: 2 Oct 2026 a** and the Denmark territorial-exclusion note. The manager's earlier XML reading showed 101; publication/cache lag had cleared by this inspection. The collector uses the configured HTML and does not let stale XML override it. Literal action codes are case-sensitive: blank suffix → ratification (**90**); `a` → accession (**4**); `A` → acceptance (**3**); `AA` → approval (**5**). Treating all 102 as ratifications would be incorrect.

Baseline/diff behavior: validate the complete table/notes, header legend, unique participants, valid dates, referenced notes and exact signatory/party totals before accepting a capture. The parser is bounded to 250 participant rows under the existing 512-KiB/request/runtime limits. Full HTML and parsed facts/notes stay in one baseline evidence row (`assessment.state=baseline`, explicitly not a model assessment). The initial finding window starts **2026-10-01**, containing the real Mozambique sample; the other **101** historic party actions create no findings or fabricated communication timestamps. Later additions/corrections use stable `untc:XXI-10:<country>` record identity and `participant:<country>` item keys, preserving original country/action/date in evidence and concise record text. Changed action/date facts become source versions rather than duplicate findings. A missing participant, vanished status/note, invalid date, inconsistent count, truncation or unsupported format withholds the whole baseline/diff and preserves accepted records. Country renames/removals and future unsupported action types need review; this is the current participant table, not complete depositary-notification/declaration coverage.

| --- | --- |
| Full baseline | `491eb0ec-63e9-4e5e-8602-2b90d4d265c1`; 149 participants, 102 parties, full HTML and participant notes |
| Current sample capture | `9061f591-c2a7-4059-aa6b-7581beb379c6`; Mozambique, **accession**, **2026-10-02**, literal suffix `a` |
| Retained record | `38855c3b-a2bf-4e14-b349-c15a24390dad`; **Mozambique — accession — 2 Oct 2026**, `treaty_status`, **High** |
| Initial observation | `21c6ad07-c4b4-4bdb-958f-718823fb8496`; validation, two captures, no model calls, honestly partial with one pending assessment |

All three runs use `validate=true`, this exact tracker ID and the October 1 window; assessment/repeat additionally target the exact sample capture. Each makes one official page request. The accepted reason is “Officially verified treaty action (accession by Mozambique), treated as high priority.” No event instant or source publication date was invented. The ordinary authenticated request without validation returns **400**, leaving the run count at **40**; Enabled remains No throughout. This completes SCOUT-16.01's deferred positive hosted disabled-source receipt/repeat check, without promoting the tracker.

Local audit snapshots are in `.cache/scout09/`; authoritative run/model accounting and raw evidence remain in Supabase.

The repeat had model access enabled but reserved/made no call. Hosted totals are now **25 trackers (five Yes / 20 No), nine records, 50 captures and 40 runs**. ## SCOUT-15 — Detect source changes before assessing findings and relevance

Status: in-progress (core, original reset/repull and four-additional-collector request complete; implemented SCOUT-08–11 paths use the shared flow, while unproven-source and full-coverage obligations remain partial)
Depends on: SCOUT-04's working HSA collector; SCOUT-01 model budgets before stage 3; SCOUT-05 and SCOUT-06 for stage 4 integration.

Outcome: Unchanged sources produce no additional stored content and no agent calls. New or changed source content enters a bounded agentic flow that determines whether it creates a finding, updates an existing finding, or adds no substantive information, and assesses its relevance to the Letter.

Initial implementation authorization (2026-10-01, accepted as checkpoint 160be36 before the live cutover recorded below): prioritize technical ChM architecture/data standards/interoperability/implementation workshops/consultations and officially verified treaty actions; retain other substantive BBNJ items at normal priority and generic advocacy/incidental mentions at low priority. Deduplicate source fields before model work. First complete HSA, all five stages below and the minimal communication path. Bounded model validation may use public recorded content within the existing shared caps; live reset/deployment/collection waits for manager review of the implementation and exact reset impact.

Historical reset boundary: the original one-time reset/repull was completed and accepted in `1bddfb0`; the instructions and metrics below document that operation and do not authorize another reset. Only records and dependent collected/assessment state were cleared; tracker configuration, history/accounting, Auth/access, migrations, other `chm` services and Ryu were preserved. Never reset quota accounting when clearing records.

Flow: **Fetch → scripted comparison with last consumed state → no updates, or changed content → complete candidate extraction and finding identity/update/relevance assessment → validation → record changes → notification selection.**


### Stage 1 — Define the comparison and relevance contract

- [x] Extend the existing HSA path in `collector/hsa.js`; retain stable post IDs, normalized URLs, content hashes, source chronology and organizer correction rules. Separate source-content equality from record-material-change detection: `material()` currently omits event title/description, so it cannot safely decide which source changes deserve assessment.
- [x] Define meaningful source fields before agent extraction: source text, title/description where supplied, organizer, links, dates, status and available structured facts. Normalize whitespace, tracking parameters and known volatile markup without erasing meaningful text or schedule changes. Missing keywords must not discard a technical ChM candidate; tracker `coverage` supplies context for assessment.
- [x] Define unchanged as **all meaningful fields unchanged**. One matching field never suppresses another field's change. Compute added, changed and removed fields; failed/incomplete extraction must not be interpreted as an authoritative removal.
- [x] Require every applicable content field in the extraction result for each record kind. Keep a supported title and valid kind mandatory; require organization, description, location, URLs and event-specific fields to be addressed with supported values or explicit `null` for missing/ambiguous information. Reject omitted applicable keys and blank-string substitutes. Use typed `null` internally for unknown text, dates and URLs; derive non-applicability from record kind. System-managed identity, timestamps and evidence metadata remain code-owned.
- [x] Establish a small reviewed example set and one shared assessment rubric. Use the proposed priorities below; keep insufficient context separate from priority and keep source authority separate from relevance.

| Priority | Proposed criteria | Intended use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| High | Technical ChM architecture, data standards, interoperability, implementation workshops and consultations; verified ratifications and important official changes | Prominent results and eligible Slack findings/changes |
| Normal | Other substantive BBNJ webinars, conferences, side events and announcements | Searchable records and applicable upcoming-event digests |
| Low | General advocacy, incidental high-seas mentions or commentary without a substantive update | Retain only needed changed-source evidence and assessment; no routine notification |

### Stage 2 — Implement the scripted gate and minimal persistence

- [x] Add `scout.records.priority` through an additive migration: nullable text constrained to `high`, `normal` or `low`, with no inferred default. Existing records remain `null` (not yet assessed); do not backfill priorities or generate notifications during migration. Add no tables; coordinate with SCOUT-06's proposed deliveries removal.
- [x] Compare each source against its latest successfully consumed state, including sources with no associated record. A match against any historical hash alone is insufficient: A → B → A is a new transition, although the previously stored A content should be reused.
- [x] On unchanged content, insert no evidence/capture/record rows, do not rewrite unchanged fields or advance record `updated_at`, and enqueue no new assessment or agent call for that observation. Record only collection health, existing last-seen metadata where required, and the no-updates result. A fully completed run with no changes uses `no_change`; failures and incomplete coverage retain their distinct outcomes.
- [x] For a real change, reuse unchanged fields/evidence and persist only the new content or field differences needed to support it. Start from existing `content_hash`, `parsed` and capture references; specify a versioned baseline/delta representation if needed to avoid copying unchanged fields. Preserve existing full captures, reconstructable source context and date evidence; update readers together if the representation changes. Use only the smallest necessary migration and add no tables.
- [x] Track source observation separately from successful assessment. Persist a changed candidate once; if assessment fails, times out or exhausts quota, resume that pending work separately from new observations, within budget and without inserting another capture. An unchanged fetch must not permanently suppress previously unprocessed work. Reuse completed assessments on ordinary repeat runs; changed assessment rules require an explicit reprocessing operation.
- [x] Continue bounded checks of previously linked sources independently of whether their parent post changed. Follow technical/official supporting links as well as event links; each fetched URL passes through this same gate. Preserve existing HTTP/page/time limits and report unsupported content or exhausted coverage.

### Stage 3 — Reconcile new/updated findings and assess relevance

- [x] Before the first model call, configure and enforce persistent request/token/retry caps using SCOUT-01's verified free service. Reuse deterministic extraction and exact identity matches first. Supply the agent only new/changed content, necessary prior context, tracker coverage, and a bounded shortlist of possible existing findings selected with structured queries; embeddings remain deferred.
- [x] Validate a structured result containing action (`new`, `update`, `supporting_evidence`, `no_substantive_change`, or `uncertain`), an existing record ID when applicable, supported field changes, priority, a short relevance reason and evidence references. Existing IDs must belong to the supplied shortlist. Treat source text as data; model output cannot establish unsupported facts or bypass source-authority rules.
- [x] Validate the complete candidate before deriving a sparse record update. In that update, omitted fields mean unchanged; an unknown value in a new source preserves an established value. Clear an established value only when explicit withdrawal/correction evidence passes the existing authority rules. Required extraction keys do not justify storing another copy of unchanged fields or inventing missing facts.
- [x] Reconcile different URLs describing the same event or announcement into one finding. Use organizer/title/date information together; dates may change on rescheduling. Keep recurring event editions, individual conference sessions and different treaty actions distinct. Uncertain identity remains pending with its reason; do not automatically merge or create a duplicate record to resolve uncertainty.
- [x] Apply supported updates transactionally and reassess the resulting finding using its combined evidence. Preserve authoritative corrections, unknown/date-only precision and official verification of treaty changes. Adding corroborating evidence alone must not advance the material record version or trigger a notification.
- [x] Store the finding's current accepted priority in `records.priority`, updating it transactionally with the assessment. Keep the supporting reason, assessment version, source fingerprint and any uncertainty state in `evidence.parsed`. `uncertain` is an assessment state, not a priority value; a failed or uncertain reassessment preserves any established priority. An insufficient-context result can request bounded supporting content, after which the same flow resumes; retain an explicit limitation if evidence remains insufficient.

### Stage 4 — Use relevance in Records and Slack

- [x] In `src/records.js` and `src/dashboard.js`, add filtering/ordering using `records.priority` and expose the supporting short reason through the restricted reader projection. Slack selection must use the same record field rather than choose between per-source priorities. Keep date ordering available and label null priority **Not yet assessed**; do not silently score or announce historical records during rollout.
- [x] Wherever record fields are displayed in Records or Slack, render missing applicable values as **Unknown** and inapplicable fields as **Not applicable** (for example, event dates on an announcement). Apply this to existing null values as well as new records, without a placeholder backfill or record-version change. Never create a timestamp or clickable URL from an unknown label.
- [x] In `notifications/slack.js`, select high-priority new findings and important material changes before applying batch limits. Use the same finding assessment for digests: eligible high/normal upcoming events, priority then date, with existing cancellation/unknown-date behavior. Preserve correction notifications for previously delivered events even if their current priority falls.
- [x] Follow SCOUT-06's minimal communication approach: let the agent use current content, assessed priority, event timing and communication recency to select worthwhile messages. Reposts, corroborating evidence and repeated assessments alone should not prompt another message; do not restore delivery snapshots, baselines, receipts or replacement chains.

### Stage 5 — Verify HSA, then reuse the pattern

- [x] Verify the priority migration preserves existing records as unassessed and rejects unsupported priority values. Check accepted priority changes affect ranking and communication selection; failed/uncertain reassessments preserve established priority, and repeated identical assessments alone do not prompt another notification.
- [x] Extend existing collector/notifier tests with meaningful cases: exact repeat and cosmetic changes create zero captures/records/model calls; unchanged title with changed date proceeds; A → B → A reuses content but applies the transition; an unchanged post still allows a changed linked page; failed/pending assessment resumes once without duplicate storage; fetch failure preserves state.
- [x] Verify omitted applicable extraction keys and blank strings are rejected, explicit unknowns are accepted for otherwise nullable fields, and known values satisfy type/enum/date-order constraints. Check Unknown versus Not applicable rendering, preservation of established values when a new source is incomplete, and evidence-backed clearing of a withdrawn value. Complete extraction must still produce only the necessary field updates.
- [x] Evaluate technical ChM content without BBNJ keywords, generic high-seas noise, conference/side-event recognition, cross-source duplicates, authoritative corrections, distinct recurring sessions and official treaty actions. Record expected/actual priority and identity decisions, missed important items, false promotions and incorrect merges before enabling assessment-driven notifications.
- [x] Once implementation is authorized, verify locally with recorded sources and injected model/Slack responses, then make a bounded live model/collection check under the configured caps. Inspect decisions before enabling real notifications. Record source counts, skipped duplicates, changed candidates, model usage, record effects and remaining limitations.
- [x] Carry the shared gate, complete-candidate validation, identity/relevance handling and durable budgets into the implemented SCOUT-08–11 collectors, with source-specific extraction/fixtures and focused commits. SCOUT-09, reviewed websites and bounded Bluesky discovery now use it; final shared-flow/source regressions total 162 passing tests/build. The original four-additional-collector request is complete.
- [ ] Complete proof for the eight No configurations and broader required source coverage; finish the remaining SCOUT-14 end-to-end obligations. HSA/Pew access, conditional configuration, pending model proof and scheduling/frontend deferrals are not closed by shared-flow adoption or fixtures. See the SCOUT-16 inventory audit.

All 41 tests, the production build and diff checks pass. Local fixture reset deletes evidence then records and repulls through assessment while preserving tracker configuration and collection/model accounting. Real PostgreSQL semantics through the hosted adapter prove a failed record transaction cannot roll back a pre-call reservation. Browser fixture checks cover priority/unassessed filters, date ordering, table/cards, direct record selection and Unknown/Not applicable labels; this is not a claim of hosted signed-in verification. The auth regression verifies a validated record UUID survives Google sign-in through tab-scoped storage, preserving the exact approved callback URL and reader access check. Direct record views offer a link back to all records.

Two Gemini attempts (no retries) consumed 1,234 input and 994 output tokens in total; each remained under 8,000/1,024. Run `cc7e9928-2f13-41b4-a071-ce25691405b6` holds durable reservations and actual usage. The earlier one-request SCOUT-01 verification was conservatively accounted once in run `2ad6c848-05b0-49c0-8d8d-ed463c3a94e6`, at reservation time rather than an invented original timestamp. **17 Scout attempts remained on the Pacific day at this checkpoint.** These accounting-only writes changed no record, tracker configuration, migration, deployed code or Slack message. | Reviewed example | Expected | Final actual | Evidence/limit |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Technical ChM study without BBNJ keywords in excerpt | New, high, announcement | Matches | First response wrongly used an event URL on an announcement; rejected before storage. Explicit field contract fixed it in the second request. |
| Generic high-seas advocacy | No finding, low | Matches | No substantive update; cached completed assessment. |

| Authoritative cancellation plus weaker technical evidence | Preserve date/status; allow normal→high relevance | Matches in injected regression | Supporting evidence can also revise priority; uncertainty preserves it. |
| Organizer reschedule and A→B→A | Apply transition, reuse evidence; reject ordinary repeats | Matches in recorded/injected regressions | HSA/Zoom precision and conflicts remain covered. |

Metrics: final live outputs have zero missing applicable keys, zero unsupported dates/URLs, no false promotions or missed high-priority examples among these four snippets. One initial inapplicable URL was caught and corrected. Three retained candidates use 20 explicit nullable unknown/inapplicable fields in total; the noise result has no candidate. Identity regressions have zero incorrect merges/duplicates in the tested cases. This is a small integration sample, **not an accuracy estimate**; conference/recurrence/correction identity coverage remains injected and must be checked on the fresh pull before real notifications.

Deploy the private function with only `index.ts`, `deno.json`, `hosted-hsa.js`, `hsa.js`, `assessment.js` and `model.js`; configure the existing Gemini key as the Scout-specific backend secret `SCOUT_GEMINI_API_KEY`. Limitations/revisit: the shortlist is three structured candidates, useful source excerpts are bounded, and large/multi-event/quoted/PDF content can remain pending or partial. An uncertain identity never gets an automatic duplicate as a fallback. Timely reminder eligibility is bounded to high-priority events within seven days after 24 hours without communication; the agent still decides whether to send. Interrupted Slack acceptance remains best-effort with the requested single timestamp. Expanded-source metrics and pending-source follow-up remain open; they are not hosting or Supabase-access blockers.

Matching migrations through `20261001160000` applied; the next migration correctly rejected 13 conflicting legacy publication links. Because the user requested discarding collected state, only those legacy dependent rows and records were deleted, followed by migrations 170000–190000 in the same transaction. Local records/evidence are now empty; tracker identities/configuration and all six run/accounting entries are preserved. The database, committed fixtures and unrelated cache were retained. - **Deployment:** final Edge version **12**, bundle SHA-256 `4b75b74a666bccb3943707e57ccd712b94b86760e5bd5158b01a8c69dd1319f5`, uses only the six reviewed source/import files and new backend-only `SCOUT_GEMINI_API_KEY`. ### Enabled contract and promotion evidence

`enabled=true` means **a working, tested collector is proven to receive and persist real source data for this exact tracker configuration**. It does not mean a heartbeat watches it, a schedule exists, the latest run succeeded, every linked document is covered, or every assessment is correct. `false` means unconfigured, not implemented, not yet proven, or no longer proven after a source/collector change. It is no longer a pause/resume setting.

Every new collector follows these steps:

1. **Inspect and bound the source.** Confirm the configured URL/account and required record types against the live source. Prefer observed feeds/APIs and structured data; use a small source-specific HTML parser only where needed. Set coverage/window, pagination, link depth, byte and runtime limits. Capture actual representative source input; no advance claim that an endpoint exists.
2. **Implement while disabled.** Reuse existing source storage, identity/deduplication, assessment and durable usage accounting. Keep original precision for dates and unknown facts. A website tracker must independently collect its configured source; a linked page fetched by a Bluesky tracker does not prove the website tracker.
3. **Verify behavior.** Use recorded source examples for initial extraction, unchanged repeat, real content/date/cancellation change, duplicate across sources, different recurring editions, and malformed/empty/failed response. Cosmetic navigation changes must not create captures/model calls. A fetch failure must preserve accepted records. Exercise only applicable cases, not duplicated boilerplate tests.
4. **Prove real receipt while still disabled.** Use the restricted validation path in SCOUT-16.01 for one exact tracker ID and a bounded window containing at least one real item. Persist a source capture and run evidence, verify extracted facts against the source and complete at least one valid assessment (a correct Low/no-record decision counts). Empty feeds, HTTP 200 alone, mocked results or wholly pending assessments are not proof. Widen a historical window deliberately if the source has no recent items; never invent data.
5. **Repeat and review.** Run a bounded unchanged repeat and verify no duplicate record/capture or unnecessary model call. Record actual limits, pending items and partial coverage honestly. If a broader run is partial, demonstrate the completed sample's repeat and explain exactly what remains unsupported; partial is never relabelled success.
6. **Promote explicitly.** In the task row/checkpoint record tracker ID, exact URL/collector, implementation commit/deployed version, sample source URL and capture ID, initial/repeat run IDs, observed facts/assessment, test result, usage, and limitations. The manager reviews that evidence, then authorizes the exact tracker update to Yes and verifies the stored flag. This is not automatic on HTTP success or test completion. If proof fails, leave No with the blocker and next step.

A change to URL/account or collector invalidates proof and requires revalidation. A cadence-only change does not. Temporary source/network/quota failures belong in collection health and do not automatically erase proof; a demonstrated broken parser/transport should be marked No until repaired and proven again. Scheduling pauses, if later required, need a separate decision.

### SCOUT-11.01 — Prove the existing Bluesky discovery tracker

Status: in-progress (**a9a84ae / v51 accepted by the manager as bounded observation-only delivery; Enabled No**). Completed live assessment/accepted priority, post-assessment repeat and promotion remain open.
Tracker: seed 21, `2f9bb18f-d925-4bf6-8ed4-8ba477c47cfc`, [BBNJ discovery search](https://bsky.app/search?q=BBNJ), `bluesky_search`.
Depends on: SCOUT-16.01; run after the source groups above, without waiting on the unconfigured conference.

The [official API directory source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/bluesky-social/bsky-docs/main/docs/advanced-guides/api-directory.mdx) documents direct public AppView access at `api.bsky.app`, with `public.api.bsky.app` preferred for caching. The [searchPosts lexicon](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/bluesky-social/atproto/main/lexicons/app/bsky/feed/searchPosts.json) allows provider-dependent authentication and warns that cursors need not exhaust results; `since`/`until` constrain `sortAt`, not necessarily publication time. Actual Scout-transport probes found cached search **403**, cached author feed **200**, and direct AppView first-page search **200** for all four queries without credentials. Direct cursor requests returned **403**; runtime does not retry them or bypass access controls. The configured `bsky.app` page is a JavaScript shell. Documentation-site TLS/502 failures were resolved by reading the official repositories without disabling TLS.

Implemented in the existing collector, with no new tracker, schema, helper, framework or dependency:

- Four separate queries: `BBNJ`, `"High Seas Treaty"`, `"traité sur la haute mer"`, and observed conference tag `BBNJCOP1`. One five-post page each, interleaved to share support capacity; publication window at most 35 days, with default 30 days. The explicit proof window begins **2026-09-01**. API/index/publication timestamps remain distinct.
- Deduplicate query overlap by AT URI; retain DID, CID, full post, queries/window and supporting HTML. Conflicting CIDs/author identity are deferred. Meaningful post/content/link changes survive; engagement counts, search order and changing query window do not create captures. One explicit support destination per post, six distinct destinations including failed attempts, with registration first and the existing request/time/byte/redirect limits.
- Discovery has **rank 0** and never promotes publishers or trackers. A single matching structured event page can supply facts; the two HSA MGR posts retain **September 29, date only**, with no invented timezone, organizer or instant. Exact post identities and corroborated registration/date match the existing HSA policy-brief and MGR findings; fixture corroboration preserves reviewed facts and rejects duplicates, incompatible sessions and date changes.
- The actual Nairobi COP12 **side-event invitation** remains an event with insufficient context and unknown organizer/schedule; no support URL was present. The WILDTRUST simulation recap remains an announcement, not an upcoming event. The Palau conservation-leadership interview supplies an unrelated/general example; its fetched article also contains treaty claims, so the recorded regression retains uncertainty rather than accepting those claims as an announcement or fabricating an event. No live relevance/Low assessment is claimed.
- Treaty claims in **both posts and fetched supporting text** require official verification, including the Actu Alt Plus article's “101 parties” and entry-into-force claims. Changing the candidate kind cannot turn those claims into accepted announcements. Missing support, unresolved identity and bounded coverage remain explicit.

The controlling user direction is: “forget the old content we are gonna trash that and start over. we are building pipeline until we are launched all content is disposable and meant for testing purposes only.” Old records, captures, assessments and collection-history/report content are therefore not migration obligations. Clearing a report does not authorize deleting that ledger. Record the actual reset scope/time with its pipeline cutover and verify the protected configuration/accounting; do not add an unsolicited reset utility. Fresh runs must retain provenance and failure evidence until intentionally cleared.

### Pipeline contract

- [ ] Fetch source documents and compare their payloads with the previous capture using a simple deterministic hash. Identical content skips reassessment; minor changes may cause reassessment. Remove only explicitly documented mechanical noise. Track successful processing separately from observation so unchanged content with a failed assessment can recover. Record collector/prompt/rubric versions and support deliberate rubric reassessment of unchanged captures.
- [ ] Extract feed/API entries or structurally identifiable posts without relevance filtering or semantic preclassification. A source document is not a potential record: keep articles/newsletters intact even when they contain several events. Preserve stable document identities, source URLs and provenance.
- [ ] For feed entries, fetch the linked article within an explicit bounded retrieval policy and retain feed metadata. Check known article documents independently of an unchanged listing. Report excerpt-only input when article retrieval fails; record unfetched supporting links and pagination/coverage bounds rather than claiming complete coverage.
- [ ] Fetch bounded supporting documents needed to complete a finding, including linked event/registration pages. Preserve document IDs, URLs, payloads, cleaned content and link relationships; supply their content together with the primary document's evidence to Gemini before final field assessment/resolution. The parser may follow structurally explicit primary/registration links; Gemini may request supplied links for missing evidence without turning the parser into a semantic eligibility filter. Use a documented one-hop policy within existing public-fetch request/byte/redirect/time safeguards, deduplicate destinations and recheck support changes independently. Record attempted, failed, unsupported and budget-deferred links in the run report. Never infer contents from a URL/label or treat an unfetched registration page as verified schedule evidence. A support-only change must make affected assessment eligible again.
- [ ] Prepare readable source content by removing HTML/XML framing, scripts and navigation while preserving headings, tables, captions, dates and link labels. Do not infer kind, relevance, importance, event boundaries or record fields in the parser. Skip whole unchanged feed documents rather than stripping previously seen sentences from a changed article. Handle long documents through explicit context-preserving limits/chunks; retain exact inputs and disclose omissions instead of silently prefix-clipping them.
- [ ] Have Gemini extract zero, one or several grounded findings with ratings, then retrieve candidate records from finding-level facts and resolve new/additional-evidence/correction/uncertain identity in a separate model stage. Retain deterministic identity/write safeguards. Keep missing evidence/confidence separate from importance. Resolve Low/Medium/High naming versus existing Low/Normal/High, and whether Low records are retained, before changing storage/UI behavior; the discussion did not settle those choices.
- [ ] Validate each proposal before saving. Return rejected output and specific validation errors to Gemini for a bounded repair loop (two additional attempts per affected stage/cycle). Preserve all responses/errors and count attempts against shared limits. Do not relax source-evidence or access checks to obtain a passing result. Exhausted repairs end in an explicit failed assessment with a recoverable queue state, not an unexplained pending label.
- [ ] Validate factual support rather than verbatim reproduction. Require resolvable citations to retained documents/passages for factual fields, allowing several compatible passages/documents to jointly support a field. Permit normalized titles/organizations, faithful concise descriptions and date/time conversion; final text need not be a literal substring, and no single quote must independently parse to the final timestamp. Retain the model's component evidence and normalization rationale for inspection. Keep deterministic shape/type/length/URL/calendar/order checks; missing year, clock or zone stays unknown unless supplied by compatible evidence. Do not substitute publication year for event year. Conflicts, unsupported additions and ambiguous identity require repair or an explicit unresolved outcome, not an invented value or overwrite. Compatible corroboration may fill unknown facts; corrections to established facts still require same-finding evidence and an explicit authority/conflict decision. Do not replace the old brittle parser with an expanding set of semantic validation regexes. Verify multi-passage schedules and faithful summaries alongside unsupported/conflicting negative cases.

### SCOUT-17 finding lookup and identity plan — approved 2026-10-04

The user approved this design and requested handoff to the manager for implementation. It replaces the document-title/source-identity lookup approach in the initial shared model stage below. Document identity is provenance, not finding identity. A report wrapper or a narrow title-match patch does not complete this work.

- [x] Separate two model stages: **extract findings** from cleaned source documents (zero/one/multiple grounded proposals), then **resolve each finding** against existing records. Retain extraction evidence and the exact inputs/responses of both stages. Parser code remains structural; it does not infer finding relevance or identity.
- [x] Retrieve possible existing records using the extracted finding's name/title, organization, URLs, dates and other supported identifying facts, rather than the enclosing newsletter/document title. Begin with ordinary database search; embeddings remain deferred. Include same-document associations as provenance hints, not cross-document identity gates. Missing dates/organizations must not silently exclude legitimate matches. Supply actual record IDs/facts to the resolution model. Report weak, overlarge or incomplete candidate sets explicitly; do not silently omit identities to fit request bounds.
- [x] Have the resolution model choose **new finding**, **additional evidence**, **correction/update**, or **uncertain match**, with grounded reasons and references to supplied candidates. Different sources describing one finding must reuse its record; different editions/sessions can share titles/URLs without merging. A disagreement about date or organizer is evidence to resolve, not proof of a new identity or permission to overwrite. Preserve deterministic concurrency/version/write guards and source-backed field validation. Do not automatically merge on title similarity.
- [x] Leave uncertain matches unresolved for inspection, preserving the proposal, evidence, candidate set and reason without creating a speculative duplicate or altering an existing record. Keep identity confidence and unresolved evidence separate from importance ratings. Revalidate and repair failed extraction/resolution outputs within bounded attempts, retaining errors and successful earlier proposals. Account for both stages and repair calls in durable quota/run limits; if context or budget cannot support resolution, report deferred/partial work rather than skipping lookup.
- [x] Make run reports expose extracted findings, retrieval candidates/coverage, resolution decisions, repairs and saved associations so a reviewer can identify where a failure occurred. Candidate retrieval and resolution are explicit operations within the generation/validation steps; keep the report simple.
- [ ] Build reviewed identity cases and measure **retrieval misses**, **duplicates**, **false merges**, and **update correctness** separately from relevance/importance accuracy. Include the reproduced Weekly digest / Research bulletin documents containing the same Ocean Data Release; same finding across changed titles/URLs; corroboration; conflicting facts/corrections; recurring editions and distinct sessions; ambiguous matches; and concurrency/repeat behavior. Verify the correct record is retrieved before judging model resolution.

The first five items are implemented locally for OCTO below, pending manager review; other adapters/live acceptance remain open. Injected identity cases are implemented; human-reviewed/model-quality evaluation in the final item remains open.

Implementation remains inside SCOUT-17's all-25/every-applicable-path scope and current disposable-test-content policy. Manager reviews focused slices and then continues migration. The earlier uncommitted draft tests in `tests/document-records.test.js` were reconciled with this approved design in the replacement checkpoint below. No new workers, heartbeat restart, embedding service, Slack send or unrelated scope is authorized.

### SCOUT-17 initial combined document model stage / OCTO connection — 2026-10-04 (superseded)

**Historical, not rollout-accepted:** this section describes 003ba5e. Manager reproduced a cross-document identity defect; the approved plan and replacement checkpoint supersede its combined contract and eight-candidate behavior.

**Manager acceptance:** `5d7ce2f` is accepted as the all-25 audit and local OCTO fetch/change/prepare checkpoint, including the nested-block and feed-offset corrections, restricted reports, 30 focused/four legacy tests and build. It was partial delivery; no collector had new-contract end-to-end acceptance. The following shared-stage assignment continues the existing full authorization. The audit fixture names are already corrected to `un-treaty.json.gz` and `dosi-roundup-captures.json.gz`; verified rather than adding duplicate fixes.

**Local implementation:** `collector/document-records.js` provides a common model stage consuming a prepared document, structural ranges/links and existing storage/trace/model adapters. OCTO opts in with `documents: 'process'`; `documents: true` remains fetch/prepare-only. Default/CLI/hosted routes remain legacy, with the hosted unknown-parameter guard unchanged. The parser still supplies no finding fields, organizer, semantic eligibility or event boundaries. The document stage never calls the legacy scripted validator/saver. The small explicit response contract has `complete`, `reason` and zero to four proposals per range, each with action, supplied record ID or null, literal finding anchor, complete candidate fields, priority, reason and field evidence. Insufficient context or additional findings must be marked incomplete. Current Low/Normal/High and new-Low assessment-only behavior are retained; unresolved naming/retention choices do not block this compatible implementation.

**Context and requests:** one serializer (`requestBody` in the existing Gemini module) measures the exact serialized body including prompt, response schema, existing records, title, source ranges, structural context and repair output/errors. The hard limit remains **8,000 UTF-8 bytes**, not 8,000 source characters or an assumed chunk-token conversion. Initial packing reserves 2 KiB for repair. Primary ranges cover the entire prepared text contiguously; heading ancestors, table captions/headers and neighboring text are supplied with disclosed omitted ranges. Table continuations retain column separators and heading context. The real MPAtlas source fits intact in one request with no omitted ranges. Oversized context/repair payloads and more than eight relevant existing records explicitly defer; there is no silent truncation or invented support content. Response output remains 1,024 tokens. Supporting-link/media retrieval and broader archives remain unfinished and labelled unfetched.

**Validation and writes:** all non-null fields have literal quotes from supplied source context; textual values must occur in their quote. Dates need a valid full date, explicit clock/zone and an independently matching quoted timestamp; date-only evidence cannot manufacture midnight UTC. Unknowns preserve known fields, non-events cannot gain event fields, and treaty-status requires the official treaty source. Existing targets must come from the supplied shortlist with matching finding identity. Changed title/organizer/schedule requires an explicit correction quote naming the same finding/new value. A source URL is not a record key: two genuine sessions from one article can create distinct records. Current-record duplicate checks, stable finding anchors and durable accepted-proposal keys prevent duplicate creation across chunks/retries/repeats. Save transactions recheck record versions and observation freshness; a new supporting source cannot overwrite established fields. Each saved association references its retained document capture and has an ordinary accepted assessment for existing reader projections. Accepted proposals and their save/recovery state commit together; a later failure does not roll back earlier valid findings.

**Repair, quota and recovery:** schema/source/save rejections retain the complete returned output and precise errors. Up to two repairs follow one generation cycle; all calls use the existing Gemini transport and durable shared reservation ledger. The original transient-5xx retry counts too. Two/minute, ten/run, input/output and deadline guards remain; no daily ceiling, paid fallback or quota reset was introduced. Pending repairs retain their exact input, rejected output, errors and next round across quota/time deferral. Exhausted cycles stop with explicit recoverable failure; a later explicit invocation may retry while retaining accepted proposals. Malformed or incomplete model text is repairable when available. Reflected API credentials are redacted in both raw response artifacts and parsed/rejected values so repair does not reintroduce them.

Processing completion is separate from observation/preparation and keyed by `document-prompt-1/rubric-1` plus preparation version. Completed unchanged content skips model use and refreshes associated observation timestamps. Changed captures, a reverted source payload, incomplete work and deliberate `documentVersion` reassessment remain eligible. Completed primary ranges and accepted proposals survive recovery. Preparation v2 adds structural table-header context and stores each preparation under its version; report references specify that version. A retained older preparation remains immutable, including reports from before explicit version references. This is evidence stability for actual runs, not legacy-corpus migration or report reconstruction.

**Reports/schema:** exact wire requests and obtainable responses, model proposals, precise errors, accepted proposals and saved record IDs remain inspectable. Recovered failures retain their original Fail detail with a `recovered_by` link; only resolved failures stop failing the aggregate step. Actual unresolved failures/partial outcomes remain visible. Pre-dispatch packing/time failures do not fabricate downstream validation/save work. The UI labels document processing separately from fetch-only and legacy routes. ### SCOUT-17 two-stage extraction and finding resolution — 2026-10-04

**Review defect / replacement:** manager did not accept 003ba5e for rollout. “Weekly digest” and “Research bulletin” could each create “Ocean Data Release” because lookup used their enclosing titles. The user-approved plan in d84fd2c replaces that combined contract with extraction, finding-level lookup and separate model resolution. This implements the approved design locally for OCTO; the earlier +65 draft test lines were reconciled into stage-specific cases.

**Extraction and retrieval:** extraction persists zero/one/multiple grounded findings before lookup, with no existing IDs or action selection in its input/output. Exact requests/responses remain in run artifacts. SQL unions title/anchor, organization/location names, quoted identity/correction terms, grounded finding URLs (including announcements), UTC start day, description and provenance hints. Missing/disputed fields are not mandatory match gates. Actual candidate IDs/facts/anchors/source and finding URLs reach resolution before its decision. Coverage includes terms, missing facts, total/returned counts and lexical limitations.

The old eight-candidate cap is removed. All candidates and evidence must fit the unchanged 8,000-byte serialized request. No ranked subset is silently sent instead. A separate 100-row database read guard records the full count and defers incomplete retrieval. Nine compact matches pass; a larger byte-bound set and 101 matches defer without resolution. Ordinary lexical search may miss aliases/paraphrases sharing no facts or return broad sets; no recall guarantee or embeddings are claimed.

**Resolution and writes:** explicit outcomes are new, evidence-only association, correction/update and uncertain. Similar titles/shared URLs never trigger automatic merges. Disputed dates/organizers cannot prove novelty or authorize overwrite. Distinct sessions/editions remain distinct, including explicit labels when dates are unknown. Corrections require same-finding evidence and origin-source authority; corroboration cannot acquire overwrite authority. Unknowns preserve established values. Evidence-only decisions preserve record facts, rating and schedule provenance; differing extracted ratings are not falsely recorded as accepted. Valid uncertain decisions retain evidence/candidates/reason with no speculative write, separate from malformed-output failure. Unchanged uncertainty is retained for inspection; changed content or explicit version reassessment revisits it.

**Durability / bounds:** both stages use the unchanged serializer and durable two/minute, ten/run ledger, 8,000 request bytes, 1,024 output tokens, one counted transient retry and at most two validation repairs per affected stage/cycle. No daily cap or paid fallback. Extraction/range progress, accepted resolutions and pending repairs survive deferral. Resolution quota failure does not repeat completed extraction; one finding's exhausted repairs do not discard earlier saves. Transactions serialize candidate recheck/write with a records table lock, compare full snapshots and observation freshness, and commit associations/progress together. Document revision checks prevent concurrent progress replacement. These are injected interleaving/rollback tests, not hosted multi-connection/performance proof.

**Reports/schema:** extraction, retrieval coverage/candidates, resolution decisions, precise repair errors and saved IDs use existing generation/validation/save operations and artifacts. The UI and pending report RPC expose uncertainty separately from failure. No new table/column/grant/migration, backfill or compatibility layer was added. Schema remains fourteen local migrations/four tables/47 columns; the report migration is undeployed. Version: document-extract-2/resolve-1/rubric-1/octo-documents-v2. Earlier preparation artifacts remain immutable.

**Verification:** **50 distinct focused tests** (20 document-stage, 12 preparation, 14 report/access/UI, four hosted boundary), production build and diff checks pass. An affected document/report rerun covered 33 existing tests; the final document rerun covers 20, including the added announcement-URL/alias case. These overlap and are not additional totals. Tests exercise actual Gemini request/reservation/response code with injected transport and PGlite. Test-only elapsed-minute simulation retains every reservation; a separate case verifies real quota rejection and counted transient retry.

Identity failures are checked independently: expected IDs are asserted before resolution; the newsletter reproduction associates two sources with one record; changed titles/URLs and missing facts exercise independent lookup signals, including announcement URLs retained in associated finding evidence and quoted aliases; a no-shared-fact lexical miss is disclosed; distinct sessions/editions resist false merges; disputes remain uncertain; corrections preserve unknown/unrelated facts; corroboration cannot overwrite; concurrent insert/update/progress and interrupted saves cannot silently duplicate or overwrite. Empty/Low outcomes, both-stage repairs/exhaustion/redaction, quota/time resume, oversized repair retention and exact repeats are covered. Real MPAtlas text reaches one full extraction request; long tables retain complete primary ranges and heading/header context. These fixtures do not measure live model identity or rubric accuracy.

Evidence: ignored .cache/scout17-two-stage-focused.log (initial 49), .cache/scout17-two-stage-final-tests.log (affected 33), .cache/scout17-two-stage-document-final.log (final 20), and .cache/scout17-two-stage-build.log. Historical 48-test results for 003ba5e were not acceptance of its identity logic.

**Remaining / handoff:** manager review, human-reviewed identity/rating cases and live quality proof, remaining applicable adapters and bounded source/model checks, plus hosted runtime/locking/storage/write-overhead verification and coordinated deployment. Broad/missed lexical candidates and conservative literal correction/edition guards can leave legitimate findings deferred/uncertain. All-25 scope remains controlling.
